-No 8381. ENTRANCE TO THE HWANGPU. AnITIONS AND ALTEATIONS TO THE AIDS TO VIGATION. NOTICE is hereby given that, owing to the narrowness of the present deep-wat channel across the Woosung Outer Bar, the following additions and alterations have been made to the aids to navigation&#39;:- A white pole, surmounted by a circular shape, from which a red light is exhibited, has been ereqted on Princes Wharf, which, whon kept in line with Woosung Lighthouse, leads midway between the Outer Bar Light-boat and the 1-foot contour of the Woosung Spit, this being the line of deepest water. The two lights in line bear S. 76* 33 W. The Woosung Light remains white from the bank of the, Yangtze to the nrhwswr of the lighthouse to S. a2* W.; it has been,altered to green from S. 22* W. to S. vv,tO wesernorth-westwardLI to red from 8. *4 5o&#39; W. to the left (or western) bank of the Woosung River. The Fort Buoy has been shifted 700 feet S. 47&#39; 40&#39; W. from its former position and moored in 1h feet at low water of spring tides; from i the Woosung Lighthouse now bears S. 87* W., distant 4.9 cables. A zo-fbot conical buoy, painte&amp;amp; in red and black vertical stripes and surmounted by a bladk spherieal cage,1ihaia eecmored in 18 feet at 1ow water of spring tides otn the north- eatern edge of the Woostng 8pit; and from it the Woosung Lighthouse bearn . 62&#39; 5W., distant io.i cables. The least water at present on the line between the. outer end ofPrneWhf d the io-foot buoy placed on the north-eastern edge of the Woosung -Spit i&#39;i et n,o the orPrirnas esyWhrfan the eastern edge of the green sector, I I feet at low water of spring tides. The Outer Bar Signals now indicate the depth of water on the line when Woosung iAght and the red,1ight on Princes Wharf are in transit. DIRECTIONS. As the narrow part of the deep-water channel across the Woosung Outer Bar is abreast of the Outer Bar Light-boat, extending from 2 cables outside to. caebles inside of her, inward- bound readels can afford to open the leadipg marks to the northward as soon as they are 2 cables inside of the light-boat. Outward-bound vessels should steer to pas cable off the Fort Buoy and then gradually come on to the line of leading marks.
CAUTIOXs There is from 2 to 3 feet less water than that signalled between the io-foot buqy off the Woosung Spit and a Point 7oo feet north of the Outer Bar Light-boat. Vessels taking iup an anchorage outside Woosung are requested to avoid so doing near the line of the Outer Bar Leading Marks. N.B.-All bearings given are magnetic.
